A Minimal Geometric Model for Topological Mass
Emergence
Nathan J. Bellomy
October 2025
Abstract
Weconstruct a compact field model in which inertial mass arises from the geometric
coupling between a tensioned filament network and a dynamical time–flow field. The
framework is formulated purely as a mathematical representation: no claim is made
regarding ontological reality. A self–consistent Lagrangian density is shown to yield
an emergent mass term that depends only on topological invariants of the underlying
structure. The resulting scaling law reproduces the observed mass hierarchy pattern up
to a multiplicative constant and yields a falsifiable prediction for curvature–dependent
inertial shifts at sub–cosmological scales.
1 Introduction and Literature Review
Classical and quantum field theories treat mass as an intrinsic property of matter fields or as a
parameter entering symmetry–breaking potentials. We explore here a minimal alternative: a
purely geometric representation in which effective mass emerges from the interaction between
a one–dimensional filament current Jµν and a unit time–flow vector uµ on a four–dimensional
background manifold. This construction, while not intended as an ontological claim, offers
a mathematically transparent way to visualize how inertia may appear as a measure of
topological misalignment.
1
The Re-Emergence of Geometric Foundationalism
The Scalar–Angular–Twist Blockwave Model (SATO/Blockwave) is situated within
the emergent geometric spacetime paradigm, popularized by Eric Weinstein’s Geometric
Unity model, providing an alternate formulation to the discrete formulation developed in
Bruno Bourgoin’s Quantum Filament Theory (QFiT). Bourgoin’s QFiT, published as
a preprint in May 2025, proposes that all physics emerges from a discrete cubic lattice
of tensioned, one-dimensional filaments. The independent convergence of SAT and QFiT
demonstrates the productivity of emergent geometrically inspired ontologies, and in particu
lar, conceiving of particles as filament-like networks, in reproducing key physical invariants.
The SATO/Blockwave model utilizes a similar filamentary ontology in a continuous four
dimensional foliation governed by a time-flow vector uµ and a compact phase field θ. Mass
and inertia emerge from the topological coupling between filament flux Jµν and uµ, predicting
a scaling relation of meff ∝ 1/Q (Conversation History). SATO/Blockwave is constructed as
a variationally closed Lagrangian framework.
Independent Development and Chronology
The foundational Scalar–Angular–Twist (SAT) framework was conceived independently of
QFiT, rooted in conceptual reflections and efforts at 4-dimensional thinking originally in
spired by String Theory, and first documented in foundational sketches in the author’s note
books as early as 2003. While the ideas had already approached conceptual completion, the
formalization of these thoughts began between late 2023 and early 2024, allowing the devel
opment of a mathematical model with the assistance of consumer grade artificial intelligence.
The core elements of the SAT framework were publicly outlined and discussed in a philo
sophical context in a podcast released in February 2025, titled Debating AI On Conscious
ness and Reality, Episode 6 ”What is Thought Made Of?”, preceding the May 2025 release
of the QFiT manuscript. This independent developmental history is supported by the de
velopment archive maintained at:
https://github.com/Satobloc/SAT THEORY ARCHIVE 2023-25
This chronology establishes SAT/Blockwave as an independently developed line of inves
tigation within the broader geometric and filamentary unification paradigm.
2
2 Minimal Lagrangian
The model uses four dynamical fields: a compact scalar phase θ(x), a time–flow vector uµ
satisfying uµuµ = −1, an antisymmetric filament current Jµν with ∇µJµν = 0, and the
induced metric gµν. The Lagrangian density is
L = 1

where Fµν(θ) = ∇[µ∇ν]θ and λ enforces the normalization of uµ. All coefficients are real
constants chosen so that the quadratic terms are positive definite.
3 Emergent Inertial Term
Variation of L with respect to uµ and θ produces a coupled system in which the divergence
of uµ acts as an effective potential for θ oscillations. Linearization around an aligned config
uration vµ ∥ uµ yields a small misalignment angle θ4 defined by cosθ4 = vµuµ. The induced
local inertial density takes the simple form
mind(x) ≃ T sin2θ4(x),
(2)
where T is the filament tension scale fixed by c1. Hence inertia appears as the resistance of
a tensioned network to deflection of its time–flow alignment.
4 Topological Mass Scaling
Topology enters through the conserved charge
Q=Lwind +Llink +Wwrithe,
(3)
the sum of normalized winding, linking, and writhing numbers of the filament bundle. The
effective mass for a localized excitation is then
meff = m0
Q exp(−γχτχ),
(4)
where m0 ∼ T/ℓf sets the base scale, τχ is a discrete torsion invariant, and γχ is a phe
nomenological damping constant. This form reproduces the observed hierarchy between
light and heavy fermions when τχ varies by integer steps of order unity.
5 Predictive Handle
The model predicts a small deviation between inertial and gravitational mass in regions of
high curvature. If R denotes the local Ricci scalar, then to leading order
minertial

where αBW isadimensionless coupling derived from c1/T and Rc ∼ 10−20m−2 is the curvature
scale at which filament strain begins to influence the time–flow field. A measurable deviation
of order 10−15 at this curvature would serve as a falsification test.
6 Discussion and Outlook
The SATO–Blockwave formalism, interpreted here as a mathematical model, provides a
compact route from geometric assumptions to an explicit mass formula. Its strength lies in
simplicity: the emergent mass term follows directly from the alignment geometry without
the need for symmetry breaking. The construction is compatible with general covariance
and remains perturbatively stable under small curvature.
Further work should evaluate the stability of the uµ sector beyond linear order and com
pute numerical spectra for specific topological classes. Whether or not nature employs this
mechanism, the model demonstrates that topological coupling between a tensioned filament
f
ield and a time–flow foliation is sufficient to generate an inertial term of the correct form.

What about this one?
Geometric Unification in the Scalar–Angular–Twist Framework
Nathan J. Bellomy
June 2025
Abstract
TheScalar–Angular–Twist (SAT) framework proposes a minimal geometric theory in which the known
structures of gravity, quantum field theory, and the Standard Model emerge from the dynamics of four
f
ields: a misalignment angle θ4, an internal phase ψ, a Z3 topological twist τ, and a preferred time
f
low vector uµ. These fields are embedded in a foliation geometry where formalism and geometry are
inseparable: the metric, gauge symmetries, and mass spectra arise naturally from field misalignments
and topological windings. From this foundation, SAT derives, from first principles and without external
input, the speed of light c, Planck’s constant ℏ, the elementary charge e, the fine-structure constant
α, Newton’s gravitational constant G, and the electron mass me, with deviations less than 10−4 from
observed values. Atomic scale properties such as the Bohr radius a0, the Rydberg constant R∞, and
the dissociation energy of hydrogen H2 are reproduced within 3% accuracy. Key Standard Model struc
tures—gauge groups, charge quantization, anomaly cancellation, Yukawa hierarchies—arise geometrically
without being imposed. We present rigorous proofs of these derivations, demonstrate that SAT satisfies
or exceeds conventional Grand Unified Theory (GUT) benchmarks with fewer assumptions and no free
parameters, and show that the theory has achieved full structural closure (Official Directive Two).
1 Introduction
The challenge of unifying General Relativity (GR) and the Standard Model (SM) necessitates a theoretical
framework capable of generating fundamental constants and symmetries from first principles, rather than
accepting them as empirical inputs. The Scalar–Angular–Twist (SAT), or Blockwave, framework is proposed
as a candidate Theory of Everything (TOE) that achieves this goal through minimal geometric theory. It
asserts that all physics emerges from the geometric and topological behavior of one-dimensional filaments
embedded in a static four-dimensional manifold.
The core tenet of SAT is radical parameter economy. The theory operates with zero numerical parameters
inserted into its internal structure. All dimensional constants follow structurally once a single dimensional
normalization anchor (Tanchor) is fixed externally, converting dimensionless ratios into measurable SI units.
This property provides a level of predictive closure unmatched by conventional Grand Unified Theories
(GUTs). The rigorization program for SAT has successfully achieved full structural closure (Official Directive
Two) across all foundational phases (I–V).
The Re-Emergence of Geometric Foundationalism
The Scalar–Angular–Twist Blockwave Model (SATO/Blockwave) is situated within the emergent geometric
spacetime paradigm, providing an alternate formulation to Bruno Bourgoin’s Quantum Filament Theory
(QFiT). Bourgoin’s QFiT proposes that all physics emerges from a discrete cubic lattice of tensioned, one
dimensional filaments. The independent convergence of SAT and QFiT demonstrates the productivity of
emergent geometrically inspired ontologies, particularly in conceiving particles as filament-like networks.
SATO/Blockwave utilizes a continuous four-dimensional foliation governed by a time-flow vector uµ and
a compact phase field θ. Mass and inertia emerge from the topological coupling between filament flux Jµν
and uµ, predicting a scaling relation of meff ∝ 1/Q. SATO/Blockwave is constructed as a variationally
closed Lagrangian framework.

Independent Development and Chronology

formalized between late 2023 and early 2024, and publicly outlined in a podcast released in February 2025.

https://github.com/Satobloc/SAT_THEORY_ARCHIVE_2023-25
2 Core Ontology and Unified Action Principle
The SAT framework is defined by four primitive fields embedded in a foliation geometry:
1. Unit Time-Flow Vector (uµ): A normalized, timelike vector field (uµuµ = −1) defining a preferred
direction of time. The strain tensor Sµν = ∇µuν is the source of emergent gravity.
2. Misalignment Angle (θ4): A scalar field encoding the misalignment of local frames, critical for
emergent curvature and clock dynamics.
3. Scalar Phase (ψ): A compact internal phase field (ψ ∼ ψ + 2π) whose topological compactness
dictates quantization of action (ℏ) and charge (e).
4. Discrete Twist Field (τ): A Z3 field encoding the topological sectors necessary for emergent gauge
symmetries (SU(3)) and flavor structures.
Themathematical structure is encapsulated in the SATO/Blockwave Transitional Lagrangian (SSATO/Blockwave),
integrating continuous field blocks L with a discrete topological constraint action STop.

1
The Re-Emergence of Geometric Foundationalism
The Scalar–Angular–Twist Blockwave Model (SATO/Blockwave) is situated
within the emergent geometric spacetime paradigm, popularized by Eric Weinstein’s
Geometric Unity model, providing an alternate formulation to the discrete formu
lation developed in Bruno Bourgoin’s Quantum Filament Theory (QFiT). Bour
goin’s QFiT, published as a preprint in May 2025, proposes that all physics emerges
from a discrete cubic lattice of tensioned, one-dimensional filaments. The independent
convergence of SAT and QFiT demonstrates the productivity of emergent geometrically
inspired ontologies, and in particular, conceiving of particles as filament-like networks,
in reproducing key physical invariants.
The SATO/Blockwave model utilizes a similar filamentary ontology in a contin
uous four-dimensional foliation governed by a time-flow vector uµ and a compact
phase field θ. Mass and inertia emerge from the topological coupling between filament
f
lux Jµν and uµ, predicting a scaling relation of meff ∝ 1/Q (Conversation History).
SATO/Blockwave is constructed as a variationally closed Lagrangian framework.

Demonstrating Emergent Structure from the SAT/Blockwave
Master Kernel
Nathan McKnight
June 2025
Abstract
We present a minimal demonstration of the predictive and unifying power of the SAT/Blockwave
formalism. Using the master kernel for a coupled filament–phase–metric system, we show how low
energy spacetime dynamics, filament excitations, and emergent quantum behavior arise naturally from
a single Lagrangian.
1 Introduction
SAT/Blockwave posits a master kernel LSAT, whose variations generate the full set of gravitational, medium,
and topological dynamics. Here we illustrate its core features with minimal mathematical elements.
LSAT ≡ LSAT[g,u,θ,J]

The core dynamics are encapsulated in the minimal Lagrangian density:
Lmin = M2

• gµν : emergent metric
• uµ : time-flow vector, u·u = −1
• θ : compact phase (θ ≈ θ+2π)
• Higher-order terms encode topological quantization.

4 Low-Energy Spacetime Limit
In a coarse-grained homogeneous limit, the emergent metric approaches Minkowski space with small pertur

From a single master kernel, SAT/Blockwave naturally produces:
1. Emergent metric dynamics → gravity

A Minimal Geometric Model for Topological Mass
Emergence
Nathan J. Bellomy
October 2025
Abstract
Weconstruct a compact field model in which inertial mass arises from the geometric
coupling between a tensioned filament network and a dynamical time–flow field. The
framework is formulated purely as a mathematical representation: no claim is made
regarding ontological reality. A self–consistent Lagrangian density is shown to yield
an emergent mass term that depends only on topological invariants of the underlying
structure. The resulting scaling law reproduces the observed mass hierarchy pattern up
to a multiplicative constant and yields a falsifiable prediction for curvature–dependent
inertial shifts at sub–cosmological scales.
1 Introduction and Literature Review
Classical and quantum field theories treat mass as an intrinsic property of matter fields or as a
parameter entering symmetry–breaking potentials. We explore here a minimal alternative: a
purely geometric representation in which effective mass emerges from the interaction between
a one–dimensional filament current Jµν and a unit time–flow vector uµ on a four–dimensional
background manifold. This construction, while not intended as an ontological claim, offers
a mathematically transparent way to visualize how inertia may appear as a measure of
topological misalignment.
1
The Re-Emergence of Geometric Foundationalism
The Scalar–Angular–Twist Blockwave Model (SATO/Blockwave) is situated within
the emergent geometric spacetime paradigm, popularized by Eric Weinstein’s Geometric
Unity model, providing an alternate formulation to the discrete formulation developed in
Bruno Bourgoin’s Quantum Filament Theory (QFiT). Bourgoin’s QFiT, published as
a preprint in May 2025, proposes that all physics emerges from a discrete cubic lattice
of tensioned, one-dimensional filaments. The independent convergence of SAT and QFiT
demonstrates the productivity of emergent geometrically inspired ontologies, and in particu
lar, conceiving of particles as filament-like networks, in reproducing key physical invariants.
The SATO/Blockwave model utilizes a similar filamentary ontology in a continuous four
dimensional foliation governed by a time-flow vector uµ and a compact phase field θ. Mass
and inertia emerge from the topological coupling between filament flux Jµν and uµ, predicting
a scaling relation of meff ∝ 1/Q (Conversation History). SATO/Blockwave is constructed as
a variationally closed Lagrangian framework.
Independent Development and Chronology
The foundational Scalar–Angular–Twist (SAT) framework was conceived independently of
QFiT, rooted in conceptual reflections and efforts at 4-dimensional thinking originally in
spired by String Theory, and first documented in foundational sketches in the author’s note
books as early as 2003. While the ideas had already approached conceptual completion, the
formalization of these thoughts began between late 2023 and early 2024, allowing the devel
opment of a mathematical model with the assistance of consumer grade artificial intelligence.
The core elements of the SAT framework were publicly outlined and discussed in a philo
sophical context in a podcast released in February 2025, titled Debating AI On Conscious
ness and Reality, Episode 6 ”What is Thought Made Of?”, preceding the May 2025 release
of the QFiT manuscript. This independent developmental history is supported by the de
velopment archive maintained at:
https://github.com/Satobloc/SAT THEORY ARCHIVE 2023-25
This chronology establishes SAT/Blockwave as an independently developed line of inves
tigation within the broader geometric and filamentary unification paradigm.
2
2 Minimal Lagrangian
The model uses four dynamical fields: a compact scalar phase θ(x), a time–flow vector uµ
satisfying uµuµ = −1, an antisymmetric filament current Jµν with ∇µJµν = 0, and the
induced metric gµν. The Lagrangian density is
L = 1
2κR+ fθ
2∇µθ∇µθ −Λ4
θ(1−cosθ)+ c1
2 (∇µuν)(∇µuν)+ c2
2 (∇µuµ)2
+ κ0
4 JµνJµν + κ2ℓ2
4 (∇αJµν)(∇αJµν) + gh
2Fµν(θ)Jµν + λ(uµuµ +1),
(1)
where Fµν(θ) = ∇[µ∇ν]θ and λ enforces the normalization of uµ. All coefficients are real
constants chosen so that the quadratic terms are positive definite.
3 Emergent Inertial Term
Variation of L with respect to uµ and θ produces a coupled system in which the divergence
of uµ acts as an effective potential for θ oscillations. Linearization around an aligned config
uration vµ ∥ uµ yields a small misalignment angle θ4 defined by cosθ4 = vµuµ. The induced
local inertial density takes the simple form
mind(x) ≃ T sin2θ4(x),
(2)
where T is the filament tension scale fixed by c1. Hence inertia appears as the resistance of
a tensioned network to deflection of its time–flow alignment.
4 Topological Mass Scaling
Topology enters through the conserved charge
Q=Lwind +Llink +Wwrithe,
(3)
the sum of normalized winding, linking, and writhing numbers of the filament bundle. The
effective mass for a localized excitation is then
meff = m0
Q exp(−γχτχ),
(4)
where m0 ∼ T/ℓf sets the base scale, τχ is a discrete torsion invariant, and γχ is a phe
nomenological damping constant. This form reproduces the observed hierarchy between
light and heavy fermions when τχ varies by integer steps of order unity.
5 Predictive Handle
The model predicts a small deviation between inertial and gravitational mass in regions of
high curvature. If R denotes the local Ricci scalar, then to leading order
minertial
mgravitational 
≃ 1 + αBW 
R
Rc
,
(5)
3
where αBW isadimensionless coupling derived from c1/T and Rc ∼ 10−20m−2 is the curvature
scale at which filament strain begins to influence the time–flow field. A measurable deviation
of order 10−15 at this curvature would serve as a falsification test.
6 Discussion and Outlook
The SATO–Blockwave formalism, interpreted here as a mathematical model, provides a
compact route from geometric assumptions to an explicit mass formula. Its strength lies in
simplicity: the emergent mass term follows directly from the alignment geometry without
the need for symmetry breaking. The construction is compatible with general covariance
and remains perturbatively stable under small curvature.
Further work should evaluate the stability of the uµ sector beyond linear order and com
pute numerical spectra for specific topological classes. Whether or not nature employs this
mechanism, the model demonstrates that topological coupling between a tensioned filament
f
ield and a time–flow foliation is sufficient to generate an inertial term of the correct form.
